ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u78	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The 75+-year care programme aims to increase the availability of care services and specialist care services and is aimed at single and elderly people, aged 75 years and over, who live in small municipalities, up to 20,000. residents. Within the framework of this programme, the municipality up to 20,000. residents may benefit from the State budget for the provision of care services and specialised care services when the municipality carries out the programme on its own when it does not commission another entity to carry out this task. The scheme provides financial support to municipalities for the provision of these services to single persons, aged 75 years and over, which fulfil the conditions for assistance under the Social Assistance Act. We presume that the program is addressed to about 1229 municipalities. What could the funds be earmarked for? Firstly, to increase the number of hours of care services or specialized care services for those who have already used these services, or to finance care services for those who have not yet been in this system, i.e. those who will be able to benefit from these services. What are the reasons for this program? One form of support for older people, which is laid down in the provisions of the Social Assistance Act, is the care services, including specialized care services. The purpose of these services is to help meet the everyday needs of life, but also to ensure contact with the environment. It should be stressed that the organisation and provision of services is a compulsory task of the municipality itself, financed by its own tasks. This programme allows municipalities to be assisted in their own task. It is also due to the increasingly noticeable and progressive demographic processes that lead to the ageing of our society and to the increase in the number of people requiring assistance in the form of caring services. In short, if a given municipality offers a given person 2 hours, we say that we add another 2 hours for that person, so that person will be taken care of for 4 hours, that is to say, we increase that number of hours. What does that tell you? Every year we see an increase in the number of people using care services. According to our analysis, in 2016 there was an increase of around 7% in rural, urban and rural municipalities compared to 2015. the number of persons who have just been granted care benefits through administrative decisions, i.e. an increase of 7% of the number of persons over 75 years of age. We also have analyses that show that the number of people aged 75 and over in the municipalities where these services are provided is about 55 thousand. people, which accounts for around 60% the total number of those persons. I would also like to say that this programme has already been announced and the call for proposals for this programme is ongoing. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u100	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen! I am in some trouble because the question is addressed to the President of the Council of Ministers, and I am entitled to answer it, but the content of the statement would suggest that this question should be addressed more to the Chief Labour Inspector. As a member of the Labour Protection Council, you know very well that the labour inspection is subject to the Marshal of the Sejm, as an independent body, it is not subject to the Ministry. That is why I am unfortunately forced to recall the rules on which we are based. The Member has already mentioned the Act on State officials and Article. 29 and Article 29 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 30, from which I will repeat the legal rules that apply to civil servants, that the working time of civil servants may not exceed 8 hours per day and on average 40 hours per week in the accepted settlement period not longer than 8 weeks. I would like to point out this: in the agreed settlement period no longer than 8 weeks. In cases justified by the type of work and its organisation, working time schedules may be used in which it is permissible to extend working time up to 12 hours a day. In these schedules, however, the working time may not exceed, on average, 40 hours per week during the agreed settlement period of not more than 12 weeks. If required by the office, a public official may be employed outside normal working hours and in exceptional cases also at night and Sundays and holidays. The provisions on extended daily working hours carried out outside normal working hours shall not apply to pregnant women and without their consent to persons caring for persons requiring constant care or taking care of children under the age of 8. I'm going to quote some parts of the article. 30 Act on employees of state offices: The schedule of working time per week and its size per week shall be determined by the Head of Office in accordance with the rules laid down by regulation by the President of the Council of Ministers. A public official shall be entitled to a remuneration or free time according to his choice for work carried out at the direction of his superior, except that his leisure time may be granted in the period immediately preceding or after his leave of absence. Public officials employed in the general offices and central public administrations in managerial and independent positions shall have free time for work outside normal working hours at night and on Sundays and holidays. The provisions of the Article shall apply. 29 and 30 Laws on employees of state offices contain specific regulations on working time in relation to the Labour Code and as separate provisions have priority over the regulations of the Labour Code. According to the article. 5 of the Labour Code, where the employment relationship of a particular category of worker is governed by specific provisions, the provisions of the Code shall apply to the scope of those provisions which are not regulated. The provisions of the Labour Code will apply e.g. to provide rest periods of 11-hour daily and 35-hour weekly rest. In the current legal state, the statutory body appointed for the supervision and control of labour law, prosecution of offences against the rights of the worker and other persons engaged in work shall be the State Labour Inspection subject to the Sejm and shall be supervised by the Labour Protection Board within the scope of the law established. It should be stressed that the State Labour Inspection has been equipped with appropriate powers and means to carry out supervisory and control tasks, as well as to enforce employers' compliance with existing legislation, such as. the right of entry into the premises and premises of the controlled entities, the request for information necessary in the course of the checks carried out, the issuing of orders, etc. These provisions can be further cited here. However, the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy does not have the means of directly influencing the application of labour law in practice or the powers to check compliance with labour law, including the employer's infringement of working time rules. However, it needs to be stressed that no action by employers in breach of the rules meets with the approval of our ministry. Within the framework of actions taken to eliminate violations of applicable law, the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy may refer matters to labour inspection, provide information, request intervention. And this is what happens, all the speeches that are addressed to the Ministry are sent to the labour inspection, requesting that the appropriate measures be checked and taken. That's it. In working mode, we were able to obtain short information from the State Labour Inspection regarding the Sejm's control. And I'll tell you just a little bit what it looked like. The Labour Inspectors of 23 February 2016 and 9, 16, 17, 18 and 21 March 2016 carried out a check at the Sejm Office on compliance with the rules on working time and selected provisions on remuneration for work. The correctness of the conduct of personal files selected at random has also been checked. The control protocol was signed on 30 March 2016. The inspection was carried out as a result of a signal of irregularities in the absence by the employer of a daily rest right lasting at least 11 hours. The examination examined the working time documentation for the period from 1 February 2013. [[♪ The bell ♪]] by 31 January 2016... Marshal, if you don't mind, I'll finish it.

We're gonna set up a firewall like this. In the Hunting Law, we make a few changes in terms of imposing an obligation for local authorities to cooperate with the owners of hunting circuits. We're talking about changes in the lease of hunting circuits. Just to make it short... We also change the proportions when it comes to money paid to a hunter for a sanitary strike. They're gonna get 80% now. instead of 70. In this specialty we also introduce exemption from work or work activities for persons who will perform sanitary discharge. We owe them great respect, and I give them such respect from this place. Ladies and gentlemen, the Law on the State Protection Service retains all good elements, while rejecting faulty solutions that have already appeared, as it were, when the Office for Government Protection was established. It was a temporary act, which was created as a result of the liquidation of the Vistula units and the connection with the Government Security Office. Now we're creating a central government administration that gets reinforcement and new competences. It's a combination of responsibility for protecting people and objects in one hand. There is a central administration, government administration similar to the police, fire department, other uniform services, which will receive tooling and legal, and practical to the real protection of people and objects. There will also be an increase in staffing status. It's a big mess that's always been upset with the government's security office. Ladies and gentlemen, the illusory powers, which have been called preventive powers, are disappearing into the Act, and we are giving the Service the operational powers of your protection. And, ladies and gentlemen, as a result of the discussion which also drew attention, the opposition also pointed out that these were not too far-reaching operational powers, operational and cognitive powers, I would like to make it very clear that the State Protection Service is receiving these powers, albeit not to such a large extent as other police services or special services. Of course, new competences combine with requirements. The Act establishes among others. incentive pay system, increased responsibility, strengthened staff, a completely different system of disciplinary responsibility and another system of rewards. Ladies and gentlemen! Terrorist threats are getting bolder in our part of the world. We have all been able to follow in the media information about various events, terrorist attacks in Europe. Recently, the media also reported how the assassination of the British Prime Minister was thwarted. These are very serious challenges, ladies and gentlemen. We must take them extremely seriously. Hence, there is a new law in the High Sejm - the Law on the State Protection Service. We, too, must respond actively to these new challenges and threats by creating a new service, the State Protection Service. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u324	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I will ask Mr Andrzej Matusiewicz to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u325	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I present my position on the print project 1871. The aim of the project is to exercise the right of defence necessary in the event of an attack related to the violation of the home mire. It refers to concepts already known from the Penal Code. Namely in the article. 193 of the Penal Code we have an idea of breaking in. It is already established in the case law and the doctrine of criminal law. In court practice, there are often cases of unjustified omission of the article. 25 § 3 of the Penal Code. On several occasions at the stage of the investigation, there are no arrangements for fear or agitation justified by circumstances, and these circumstances result in a failure to comply with the penalty. The circumstances in question are the way of defence, the way in which the assassination was resisted and the instrument of the assassination. There are doubts as to whether the attacker had the right, as required by the Article. 25 § 3 Penal Code, feel frightened or agitated by the circumstances of the assassination. The situation is different when there is such an event at home, in a living room, in front of the house where the resident has the right to feel safe, it is both the owner and the household. And this is a very pro-Citizen change, so that everyone in their home can feel safe. The protection of private life, the protection of family life, the protection of home mira, the provision of safety related to staying at home or at home require that such criteria as fear or uproar should not be assessed. This project differentiates the situation of the person attacked at home from the situation of the person attacked elsewhere, e.g. on the street. Events of direct rebuttal of an unlawful and actual attack must be preceded, as defined in the proposed standard of Article. 25a § 2 Penal Code, breaking into the premises, the apartment, the house, the adjacent fenced area, unless it was necessary to cross the border of defense. Infringing into case law and literature means breaking certain safeguards. In one of the court decisions it was qualified to break into the apartment by pressing the door handle and entering the apartment, which was not even locked. Breaking in means that the perpetrator behaves against someone else's object contrary to the will of the owner, acting in an unlawful manner, not e.g. in a state of higher necessity. If the boundaries of the defence need to be exceeded, the defender shall not benefit from the protection resulting from the provisions of the Article. 25 § 2a, which is the subject of an amendment, and can then be prosecuted. The draft amendment to this article is a good change in the Penal Code. The Law and Justice Club supports this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This is also guaranteed by the provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure concerning preparatory proceedings, according to which the prosecutor determines each time for a given case whether there are grounds for excluding criminal liability from the person who defends his home, apartment or property from assault. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u349	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for all your questions. It is not difficult to imagine that this type of cold calculation is simply impossible in circumstances of an attempt on your own home. What's the connection, ladies and gentlemen, the following three stories? Four thugs break into the house, hang out with a householder, and when they get away, they go after him and try to strangle him. The victim in panic grabs a knife, kills one of the criminals, and two wounds. Next thing you know, 15 appendectomies come up to the property, one jumps over the fence, threatens to kill the owner, tosses him, he's drunk. The owner in defense hits the assailant in a harmless way. And the last situation: assaulted in his own apartment mortally wounds the intruder with a knife in his belly. All of these situations took place on the property or in the premises of the person who was assaulted. In all these situations, it seems natural that the defender acted under fear or agitation, justified by circumstances. However, none of them applied the current Article. 25 § 3, which some of you referred to here, as the one who is the antidote for all problems. I will remind you of its content: There is no penalty for who exceeds the limits of the defence necessary under the influence of fear or agitation justified by the circumstances of the assassination. Why hasn't this recipe been applied? Because of this simple reason, it requires an examination of the subjective attitude of the person defending himself, which is impossible. And yet there should be a certain presumption here: If I am attacked in my home, on my property, it is natural that I act under fear. The French understand this, the Belgians, the English, who have introduced the relevant rules in their countries, and it is high time that we also introduced such regulations. It's quite natural and obvious. That is why the arguments, Mr Critics - we know them - are not justified, that this should apply to the situation, for example. on the street. The situation on the street is very dynamic and here all kinds of attempts to objectively recognise this situation are doomed to failure. We introduce an objective criterion, which is: the premises, the property, the fenced area. Where do these criteria come from? From the article. 193 Penal Code, which is already in force today. I will remind you of this: Whoever enters another's house, apartment, place, room, or fenced area or does not leave such a place, is liable to a fine, to a sentence of imprisonment or imprisonment for a year. The protected legal good here is home mira, and therefore these criteria, which are already accepted and known in the case law, are also introduced into our provision, so as not to surprise those defending themselves, that these criteria, which have already been dealt with several times in case law, are also applied in this case. I'll remind you of one of the decisions we've been examining. The court wondered whether a garden gazebo was such a room with a fenced area, apartment, house or place. In this particular case, the Court of First Instance held that no. The garden gazebo of this type does not meet the criteria. On the other hand, undoubtedly, these circumstances that we are introducing are based on the established case law, on criteria known, tested, nothing new. Any new birthmark would cause exactly the dangers you've been talking about. Mr Mirosław Pampuch asked how many cases there were. Mr President, we will reply in writing because we must ask for data from the National Public Prosecutor's Office. We don't have that kind of information. Mr Grabowski asked about other provisions that we are introducing to ensure safety. These are, above all, provisions for the protection of victims of rape. I would like to point out that these solutions really need to change. That's the subject. for another discussion. On the other hand, the current wording of the article. 197 The Penal Code and a number of other laws, a number of other provisions actually need to be amended. It also goes on, among other things. about these laws, but also about amending the Penal Code on crimes against life and health. That should actually change. Both Polish case law and regulations are among the most lenient in Europe. This is indicated in the justifications for these projects. The rape project is already at an advanced stage of legislative work, and these are all at a conceptual stage. We are working on some of them within the Justice Institute. Mr Krzysztof Truskolaski asked for the current provision... I'm sorry, but I didn't really notice the question. If you would be kind enough to repeat it. Excuse me. Mr Andrzej Gawron is asking about the gross crossing of the necessary border of defence. Mr President, this is necessary. This clause is necessary in order to prevent the situation, I will say, from killing a child who enters the garden and breaks the fruit. Such situations absolutely deserve condemnation. You can't abuse the necessary defense. Defense is a necessary tool for protection, but it does not serve abuse. If this had not been the case, there might have been a situation where the defence would have been unlimited and its abuse would have led de facto to the defence necessary without borders. That is why this clause is necessary. Is she undefined? Like most general clauses, absolutely. Therefore, the prudence of case law and common courts is absolutely on the ground. I believe that this clause will succeed in the caselaw as a security failure in the case of this type of situation when someone overcomes someone else's fence, someone else's fence, maybe a park, maybe a small park. You can imagine a situation like this that someone wants to rip the fruit out of someone's orchard, and the owner pulls out a rifle and shoots it. Such situations absolutely deserve condemnation and no one defending himself in such a situation will justify them. That's why we need that safety hatch. Will the recipe work backwards? It's not gonna work backwards, it's gonna work in the future. Clarifying the boundaries of the necessary defence. Mr Meysztowicz has requested this type of regulation. I repeat, in the article. 193 is already sufficiently defined the limit of home mire as an object of protection contained in this provision. Here we also apply it, nothing new. The question from Mr Furgo is, what if we invite someone home and then they are attacked by the householder as a result of the conflict. These regulations, ladies and gentlemen, are not the way to kill a mailman, colloquially speaking. Why? Because there's not gonna be any breaking in. The intruder element is an immanent feature of these solutions - who enters the apartment, the premises, the house or the adjacent area. This entry element must be present here. How? It doesn't matter if this intruder is e.g. entering through the door, breaking this door, breaking in. It doesn't matter if the assailant comes in to steal the jewels or to violate someone's life, health, property. It should not matter to the person who resists such an attack, to the person who defends himself. He should not be burdened with recognizing what good the attacker wants to violate. It's impossible. It is impossible for a householder to decide what good this person, whom we encounter on our property or in our apartment, intends to violate. Whether it is our property, whether it is the property of a third party, one of the householders, our health, or one of our family's lives. It doesn't matter. Therefore, this element is necessary here. Our analysis of the case law shows that there are various situations in which a householder leaves, hearing an event, hearing someone in the hallway. Then he goes back to his apartment, the door closes, but doesn't lock it, just the door handle. Then he turns around, walks, goes back to his apartment and gets attacked in the back. He grabs a lying knife and kills his assailant with a knife. This is a specific situation that has been faced with long-term legal proceedings. The perpetrator, I'm sorry, defending himself, but as a result, the defendant was exposed to long-term legal proceedings, including a conviction. That's the kind of situation we'd like to avoid. That's what these laws are for a defender to be comfortable with, that he doesn't have to wonder if he's gonna use a vase in the hallway, a knife, a fist, or some other weapon of defense. It shouldn't matter in this particular situation. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u350	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u351	High Seym! Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Penal Code Act. The problem of the necessary borders of defence is systematically returned to the public debate in Poland. Especially when the media depicts people who have been attacked by a criminal and have theoretically exceeded the limits of the defence they need. There can be no situation in which a person victimized by an assassination attempt on her well-being, life or property will be liable to trial or prosecution. A system in which the criminal is the only real guilty and the limits of the necessary defence should be applied very widely is necessary. A citizen can't be afraid of his intervention, he can't be afraid that he's about to be charged with charges, charges for defending himself, his family, his home. It is unacceptable to prosecute persons who, by defending themselves or their loved ones against criminals, use all available means. It is the criminal who shows intent to harm, there can be no other interpretation. It should be recalled that the right to defend itself is a fundamental human right. This institution has been operating unchanged since the previous 1969 Penal Code. Unfortunately, in Polish criminal law, the limit of the necessary defence remains undefined in an exhaustive way, leaving too many interpretation possibilities in a specific factual state. The current regulation states that he does not commit a crime, who, in defence of the necessary, resists a direct, unlawful attack on any good protected by law. In the event of a breach of the defence limits necessary, in particular when the perpetrator has applied a manner of defence disproportionate to the danger of an assassination, the court may apply an exceptional relaxation of the penalty and even waive its imposition. The aim of the project is to add a provision providing for the repeal of the criminality of the ex-cendent's act, where the border of the defence necessary is exceeded by resisting an unlawful attempt to break into an apartment, a premises, a house or a fenced area adjacent to it. These new conditions, in my opinion, will improve the protection of citizens. However, further legislative solutions are needed to give greater guarantees to Poles, so that they do not have to wonder whether the prosecutor will soon knock on them with a warrant for arrest for crossing the necessary borders, which in fact means defending himself and his loved ones. The draft committee only accepted editorial amendments which raise no doubts. The Polish People's Party Club supports the amendment of the Penal Code.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u357	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act and some other laws. The project concerns the implementation in the Polish legal order of Directive 2014/41/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 3 April 2014 on a European Criminal Investigation Order in order to ensure an efficient and effective system of mutual legal assistance in criminal matters between the Member States of the European Union in the field of evidence activities by introducing the possibility of issuing a European Investigation Order. The European Investigation Order will allow courts and prosecutors or other investigative bodies to request evidence from another Member State of the European Union. For example, you won't have to bring a witness into the country to question him. Similarly, the countries of the European Union will be able to apply for proof to Poland. The European Investigation Order may be issued to protect traces and evidence of crime against loss, distortion or destruction. What is extremely important, the European Investigation Order will be able to be issued before the formal initiation of the preparatory procedure, at the examination stage, as well as in the operational and exploratory activities. In the course of such activities, it shall be issued by the authority leading it and approved by the prosecutor. The Court of First Instance will have to approve the European Investigation Order when, in accordance with the applicable rules, the investigation exercise falls within the jurisdiction of the Court, for example. It's an operational control order. The European Investigation Order will be inadmissible if it does not require the interest of the judiciary and if the Polish law does not allow the carrying out or obtaining the evidence in question. So far, there's been a two-step procedure. First, the State had to request the protection of the proof and then a separate request for it to be transmitted. The rules were complicated, and the procedure itself was reluctant to apply. The solutions proposed in my assessment will facilitate international cooperation at the level of the criminal process, which is of great importance in relation to the nature of modern crime, which does not know barriers and is increasingly cross-border. The speed of criminal response in this situation plays a fundamental role in the effective enforcement of the rights of the parties to the proceedings, in particular those who are victims of crime. The project appears to be significantly closer to achieving these objectives. This is why Kukiz15 supports changes in the proposed shape. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The aim of the Directive is to ensure an efficient and effective system of mutual legal assistance in criminal matters between EU Member States in the field of evidence activities by introducing the possibility of issuing a European investigation order. Interestingly, the EIO was introduced by the April 2014 Directive and the deadline for its implementation expired in May 2017. You have time to destroy the Constitutional Court, the Supreme Court, the National Judicial Council, and for legal implementations that will improve the functioning of the system, you no longer find it. The European Investigation Order is based on the principle of mutual recognition, according to which each EU Member State is obliged to recognise and execute requests from other countries as if this request came from its own authorities. Thanks to the END, Polish courts or prosecutors will be able to apply to another Member State of the European Union for proof - i.e. the hearing of a witness or the transmission of documents - which is in that State. Similarly, EU countries will be able to request evidence from Poland. Although there is considerable doubt here that the prosecutor's office controlled by Minister Ziobra and the slowly destroyed Polish judiciary will want to cooperate with their European counterparts. Western institutions are not destroyed, they are independent, democratic in contrast to central planning from Nowogrodzka. Nevertheless, the club The Polish People's Party supports the amendment of the Code of Criminal Procedure Act.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u362	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Government's draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure and certain other laws introduces into the Polish legal order a Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 3 April 2014 on a European Criminal Investigation Order. The EU Directive introduces an effective mechanism relating to mutual legal assistance in criminal matters between the Member States of the European Union as regards evidence by means of the possibility of issuing a European Investigation Order. Thanks to the new regulations, the Polish court, prosecutor's office or other investigating authority, e.g. The police, the Border Guard, the Customs-Scarb Service, will have the right to request any EU Member State to execute an investigation order. It should be stressed that the issuing of this order by a law enforcement authority other than the prosecutor will require the approval of the prosecutor. The issuing of a European Investigation Order may involve the carrying out of various types of evidence, i.e. activities such as: hearing, examining, familiarising with documents, checking and recording telephone calls, providing information on bank accounts and transactions in those accounts. At the same time, EU countries will be able to ask Poland to carry out evidence on our country's territory. However, there will be a possibility of another action if the requested action would be inadmissible from the point of view of the internal law of the requested State. The draft government amendment of the Act also allows the possibility of issuing an investigation order prior to the formal initiation of the preliminary investigation procedure, at the stage of the investigation procedure and operational and exploratory activities carried out on the basis of separate provisions, e.g. the Police Act, the Internal Security Agency and the Intelligence Agency Act. This will enable the investigating authority to obtain the evidence or information necessary to decide whether or not to initiate the investigation. Until now, legislation has allowed cross-border applications for legal aid, but the rules have been very fragmented and complex. As a result, they were reluctant to use them in practice, leaving this sphere unregulated. The European Investigation Order provides for a specific timeframe for carrying out the requested action and strictly limits the possibility of refusal. Greater communication and faster evidence-taking will positively increase the effectiveness of criminal proceedings conducted in Poland. The introduction of these regulations will also allow for better and more effective cooperation with the countries of the European Union in prosecuting criminals. It should be stressed that the European Investigation Order will replace the existing proceedings on the protection of evidence only in relations with those States which are bound by the Directive. Thanks to the legal regulations used in the Directive, the Polish court or body conducting the investigation will be able to request another EU country to provide evidence in that country. During the work on the above draft law in the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, it was stressed that the proposed draft law adapts Polish law to the EU law relating to the European Criminal Investigation Order and as a draft in the version presented after the symbolic changes is acceptable. Therefore, while aware of the need for legal changes to prosecute criminals, as Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, we will vote in favour of this law.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u364	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Act sets out the purpose and scope of use and the way in which the grant is granted from the State budget. Okopowa, formerly ul. Goose. In the explanatory memorandum to the Act we read: The Jewish cemetery in Warsaw is one of the most important testimonies of the centuries-old presence of Polish Jews in the territory of the Republic. By the regulation of the President of the Republic of Poland of 2014 the Jewish necropolis in Warsaw was entered together with other religious cemeteries of Powazek in the list of historical monuments. The cemetery covers an area of 33 hectares and contains more than 80,000. tombstones and tombstones, among others. Insurgents of November, January, soldiers of the Polish legions Józef Piłsudski, participants of the Polish-Bolshevik war, war 1939 and insurgents of the Warsaw ghetto. The cemetery is the largest concentration of graves of Cadians and Jewish scholars in Poland. It can be said, that next to Warsaw's Powązek, Krakow's Rakowice, Vilnius's Rossy and Lviv's Łyczak Jewish cemetery in Warsaw is one of the most important Polish cemeteries, which is of great importance to the Jewish community in the world. Keeping the cemetery in good condition and providing proper care for the conservation tombstones exceeds the possibilities of a small Jewish community in the capital. The activities of the Cultural Heritage Foundation in 2015, although the largest in the history of the cemetery, are not sufficient and require systematic support. The Foundation conducts works in the cemetery on the basis of a contract concluded with the Jewish Religious Community in Warsaw, which has empowered the Foundation to carry out all the works specified in the Act. This Act is intended to help ensure the constant professional care of the cemetery and the tomb monuments preserved on it. Thanks to the financial commitment of the Republic of Poland, Poland will save a material souvenir from the exterminated Jewish community and confirm its current policy to protect the memory of the victims of World War II. So much for the justification of the bill's applicants. The Committee on Culture and Media carried out its first reading on 6 December this year and examined the bill. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u396	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! The first reading of the government draft law on amending the excise tax law was held on 9 November this year. The essence of this draft law is the introduction in the Act of 6 December 2008 on excise duty regulation on excise duties of two new categories of excise goods: electronic cigarette liquid and so-called. innovative products. These products are not covered at Union level by harmonised excise duties as regards the structure and rates of excise duty, as well as the production and movement of excise goods. After the first reading, the bill was sent to the Committee on Public Finance and this one sent the project to the Permanent Sub-Committee to monitor the tax system. At the meeting of this subcommittee there was a wide, multi-hour debate involving, among others, representatives of the producers of these articles and other social parties. Several legislative and substantive amendments were introduced during the process of the draft law. The most important changes concern Article. 2 of this draft law, which introduced the zero excise duty provision - the e-cigarette projects concerned - in force until 31 December 2018. This change was well accepted by small operators, small producers of e-cigarettes and special products, because it is the time that these entities need to adapt to the new provisions of the Act. The Committee on Public Finances considered at its meeting on 6 December this year. the draft law supplemented by amendments adopted by the subcommittee to monitor the tax system and adopted the draft law without amendment in the vote. I therefore recommend to the High House that the draft law annexed to the print report No 2093 be adopted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u397	The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Please take the floor on behalf of the Law and Justice Club by Mr Jerzy Gosievski.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u398	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the report in Parliamentary Printing No 2093 on the report of the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending the excise duty Act, print No 1963. The proposed bill provides for the introduction of two new categories of excise goods: electronic cigarette liquids and novelty products in the excise legislation. To date, electronic cigarettes and electronic cigarettes, regardless of their nicotine content, have not been tobacco products within the meaning of the European Directives nor have they been covered at EU level by harmonised excise duties on the structure and rates of excise duty. Today, a large number of European countries have introduced or are planning to introduce taxation of liquids for electronic cigarettes. The proposed bill provides for the inclusion of electronic cigarette liquids, regardless of their nicotine content, in the excise tax system. The proposal also provides, as in the case of tobacco products, for electronic cigarette liquids to be covered by the obligation of excise marking. It should be stressed here that any producer will always be able to produce electronic cigarette liquid outside the tax warehouse, using an excise prepayment institution, which is particularly important for small and medium-sized enterprises. Like electronic cigarettes and liquids for these cigarettes, novelty products are not covered at Union level by harmonised excise duties on the structure and rates of excise duty, as well as the production and movement of excise goods. At present, a very large group of European countries have introduced the taxation of innovative products: Portugal, Italy, Romania, Slovenia, Serbia, Latvia, Hungary, Greece, Croatia, France and the United Kingdom. The next countries are considering introducing a tax on innovative products. Since novelty products are a substitute for traditional tobacco products containing tobacco for smoking in their composition, this draft law provides for the inclusion of such products in the excise tax system. With regard to novel products, the project provides for similar solutions as for electronic cigarette liquids. Innovative products, like tobacco products, will also be subject to excise marking. The amendments introduced during the work of the subcommittee, accepted during the work of the Committee on Public Finance, specify the terms in the Act. An important indication is the amendment to Amendment 5 to the Excise Tax Act, which is added to the article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a on innovative products we have clarified the identification of taxpayers who will bear joint and several responsibility for tax obligations. Such a taxable person shall be a legal person and an organizational unit without legal personality which produces excise goods and any other person who participates in their production. Another important and positive amendment, this time in Amendment 14, is that the added article. 99b of the excise duty law is supplemented by paragraph. 6, which states that in the event of the purchase or possession of electronic cigarette fluid outside the excise duty suspension procedure, if excise duty has not been paid on that liquid in the amount due, and that, as a result of tax checks, customs-credit checks or tax proceedings, it has not been established that the tax has been paid, double the rate is applied, and therefore it is 1 PLN instead of 50 gr for each 1 ml of electronic cigarette liquid. During the work of the subcommittee and later of the Committee on Public Finance, we have introduced, and rightly so, legislative amendments. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice [[♪ The bell ♪]] supports the report on the government draft law amending the excise duty law contained in Seym printing No 2093. Thank you so much for your attention. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u399	Thank you very much. Mrs Marta Golbik, Civic Platform Club. You're welcome, my fellow Member.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u400	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the opinion of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the report of the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending the excise duty Act. Ladies and gentlemen! Let me remind you of the words of Minister Morawiecki: small and medium-sized companies are the eye in our heads. We have just had an example of concreteising the Minister's words and unfortunately there is a dissonance. This is another example of how unfortunately you ignore the demands and interests of small and medium-sized enterprises. The voices of representatives of the electronic cigarettes and novelty products industry are jammed with the real purpose of the proposed law. Namely, contrary to the claims of the designers, that the bill is orderly and its ratio legis is market regulation, your approach does not leave any illusions that the purpose of the project is strictly fiscal. This is clearly demonstrated by the extremely high rate of excise duty of 50 gr per ml and similar to novel products. From the point of view of sound legal and tax policy, it would be appropriate to introduce an initial rate at a lower level, within a limit of 10 gr per ml, which we will propose in an amendment which we as a parliamentary club today will make and then gradually increase according to the behaviour of the market. On the other hand, if you have to introduce a new tax, it is our call to rationalise it so that it does not crush or kill the legal market for electronic cigarettes and novel products. The excessive rate of excise duty and, above all, the excessive increase in prices will cause users to become discouraged and the industry will shift consumption to the grey zone and completely lose control of the market, and let us point out that this industry is vulnerable to activities in the grey zone. There is also a risk that users of e-cigarettes or novelty products will be supplied in countries that have not introduced this tax, which concerns Bagatela, 20 countries of the European Union, including all Polish neighbours, or the risk of buying products on websites registered in border areas. After all, there is always a way to avoid or circumvent burdensome financial obligations. It would also be necessary to simplify the way excise duty is collected, in particular as regards excise duty on novel products, for the purpose of calculating quotas so as not to complicate the tax system. The positive direction of the changes made within the committee is the deferral of excise duties from Liquids to e-cigarettes and from innovative elections by applying a zero rate until 1 January 2019. This procedure will in some way allow businesses more time to implement appropriate technological mechanisms, including the creation of a tax warehouse and the preparation to bear a new tax burden. However, it should be borne in mind that for entrepreneurs the amendment is a huge technological revolution. It involves, among other things. with the need to carry out technological changes in production technology. For larger players on the market such changes will not be as severe as for small and medium-sized entrepreneurs who will have to invest giant sums in order to fully adapt machines and production halls to new solutions. Shock therapy, ladies and gentlemen, will fail here. Believe that the level of excise duty that we want to propose in today's amendments will avoid negative consequences. It will not cause such perceived perturbation in the young market for e-cigarettes and novelty products, which are even younger, keeping consumption in the legal zone. The policy of small steps and the gradual increase in the rate will allow the market and its participants to adapt to the new fiscal burden. Otherwise, the possibility of avoiding tax will be so wide that effective excise collection will become impossible, as was the case in the shampoos of the countries mentioned, which were also mentioned today in this debate, such as Italy or Portugal, where the assumptions of excise duty collection were significantly higher than what was ultimately achieved. In view of the above, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform makes amendments to introduce excise duties for each 1 ml e-cigarette liquid of 10 gr and for novelty products of 28,26 PLN per kg and 6,28% weighted average retail selling price of tobacco [[♪ The bell ♪]] and the allocation of funds from excise duties on electronic cigarettes and novelty products to finance health protection. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u425	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the excise duty Act. The new amendments will introduce arrangements for excise taxation of two new categories of excise goods: electronic cigarette liquids and novelty products. Until now, electronic cigarettes and electronic cigarette liquids have not been treated as tobacco products. The definition of electronic cigarette is only provided for in Directive 2014/40/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 3 April 2014. According to this definition, an electronic cigarette is a product which can be used for the consumption of a vapour containing nicotine by mouthpiece or any component of that article. The proposed law introduces new definitions: electronic cigarette liquid and electronic cigarettes. Electronic cigarettes are a substitute for traditional tobacco products. Therefore, this draft law provides for the inclusion of electronic cigarette liquids, irrespective of their nicotine content, in the excise tax taxation scheme. A similar excise duty is already in force in countries such as Italy, Portugal, Romania, Latvia, Slovenia, Finland, Greece, Croatia, Hungary and Serbia. Like e-cigarettes and e-cigarette liquids, novelty products are not covered at national and EU level by harmonised excise duties on the structure and rates of excise duty, as well as the production and movement of excise goods. Since novelty products are a substitute for traditional tobacco products containing tobacco for smoking in their composition, this draft law provides for the inclusion of such products in the excise tax system. This project introduces a definition of novel products and includes these products in excise goods. Thanks to the proposed changes to the budget, it is expected to affect more than PLN 75 million per year. In addition, changes to the rules are intended to ensure higher standards of health protection and to increase consumer safety. The Circle of Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of the above. Laws.
