ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u427	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! It is my honor to present to you the draft laws that you listed as marshals, from the package of laws, so-called. the business constitution. They are central elements of the legal and institutional reform of the business environment and their business activities. At the same time, they are the implementation of the announcements made in the Responsible Development Plan and the Responsible Development Strategy adopted by the Council of Ministers. These projects are governed by the general principles for the taking up, pursuit and termination of business activities in Poland, strengthening the main guarantees of freedom and the rights of entrepreneurs. The first question, and basically the questions that we have asked ourselves when we join the work, is whether entrepreneurs should have their system bill, and if so, how to ensure that it will fulfil its role. The answer to the first question was positive, while the answer to the second question is already, I would say, very difficult. I say this, also by looking at our system of sources of law, in which, apart from the law, every law is equal to another law. Therefore, in order to meet this in the conditions that we have when it comes to e.g. about the lack of possession by the Polish legal system so-called. organic laws, we have tried primarily to ensure that the Law of Businesses has a central role through the proper construction of this law. And maybe I'll give you a few sentences about this aspect, if you don't mind. The project was created primarily on the basis of talks with entrepreneurs, but also on the basis of own observations concerning the functioning of the administration. We have tried to transfer solutions to the ground that will help to respond to the problems associated with, among others. with the application of the law, because the sphere of application of law in practice is at least as important as the law established. In my opinion, this is the greatest difficulty, the biggest challenge facing this project. From a formal point of view, the projects are to replace, firstly, the 2004 Law on freedom of business and, secondly, the Law on the provision of services in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The first of these laws is the basis for doing business in Poland today, but I think that as a result of the amendment, which is such an important aspect in the case of readability, which we have already had about 90 since their adoption, it has become less transparent, little readable, and some of the solutions that are foreseen in it are already far from dynamics of economic processes. In these discussions on the need for change, of course, it was important for us to have the voice of entrepreneurs themselves. For example, what they point out is that the Freedom of Business Act is already a fragmented law. I think it is, among other things. by numerous changes. What is important from the point of view of practice is that it is unfortunately ignored in practice. Another flaw in the Freedom of Business Act is the lack of regulation of many legal institutions felt by entrepreneurs as needed and useful. This includes, for example, guaranteeing the institutional rights of entrepreneurs or wider guarantees of rights deriving from the Constitution. Today, the Freedom of Business Act is also characterised by unnecessary overregulation. Why? There are many detailed technical, implementing solutions, which can be successfully and even useful for greater transparency, and should in principle be regulated in separate laws while maintaining in this leading law - today is s.d.g., and tomorrow we would like the right of entrepreneurs - only such basic structures of regulation of certain legal institutions. All this means that the Freedom of Business Act has lost its value in a central act in the field of business, especially in this area of economic, public law. Of course, what entrepreneurs have been complaining about, as we have known for years, is the question of understanding rules, difficulties in dealing with offices, administrative barriers. Also for these reasons there is a need to replace s.d.g. a new legal act better suited to the current expectations of entrepreneurs. This package, and in particular the Business Law Act, as I said earlier, is the answer to this challenge, namely the practice of law enforcement or the construction of monitoring of economic legislation. In view of these problems identified by entrepreneurs and the assumptions I mentioned, the Minister of Development set up a team for the development of the so-called project in March 2016. the business constitution, which included prominent representatives of the Polish science in the field of economic law, but also practitioners of economic life. Initial projects in the legislative formula were presented in November. They were welcomed by entrepreneurs, for example at Congress 590 in Rzeszów, who pointed out that they meet their needs, and of course they do not solve all the problems that entrepreneurs face every day. We conducted such a very reliable, broad, inclusive public consultation and inter-ministerial arrangements, and not only in Warsaw. We also talked about this with entrepreneurs outside Warsaw. In general, we had quite a positive feedback, but these consultations also helped us to improve some solutions, to clarify. The proposed package contains five draft laws that your marshal mentioned. The distribution of provisions in five legal acts linked to each other structurally and substantively is intended to give them a better, more transparent systematics and to organise them properly. This procedure also makes none of the projects unnecessarily long and excessively complicated in interpretation or in legislative terms, which is also the implementation of the requirement of conciseness, but it will also allow to preserve, I hope, especially in the case of the Law of Entrepreneurs, such a stable structure for the future, so that it does not have to be subject to many subsequent amendments. The main objective of the package is to fully implement the constitutional principle of freedom of economic activity and other constitutional principles that are relevant to entrepreneurs and their business activities. With regard to the aim of the general principle, namely freedom of economic activity, it aims to create more effective guarantees and safeguards for the use by entrepreneurs of this freedom recognised by the Constitution as one of the pillars of the social market economy and at the same time as the basis of the economic system of the Republic of Poland. The principle of freedom of economic activity imposes on public authorities a number of specific obligations generally aimed at ensuring that individuals have the greatest possible freedom in the field of management and that they are able to actually exercise that freedom. In this context, the legislative package aims at putting into practice these constitutional obligations of public authorities in the field of economic activity. The achievement of these objectives requires both the reconstruction of law and, and this is certainly not less important, a change in the practice of applying and monitoring economic law. The improvement of this state requires, of course, laborious work at the core, but, what is also the basis of this law here, we believe that this state can also be improved precisely through thoughtful customs regulations, speeding up certain processes. After all, objectively looking at the functioning of the administration, the functioning of economic life is precisely in contact with you, this situation is improving over the years, but it is precisely about adding an additional impetus here as well. We would also like to see these laws as a result of supporting the process of changing the attitudes of the administration, but also building the confidence of businesses in the law. After all, it is not the task of entrepreneurs to follow case law or read into the comments to laws. The achievement of these objectives also requires that I say a two-track approach: on the one hand, systemic changes, horizontal changes, which will penetrate the entire economic law and will generally affect entrepreneurs, and on the other hand, because, as I said at the beginning, it is certainly not enough action, concrete changes, point changes, removing unnecessary burdens or ambiguity, and sometimes even legal absurdities, but also creating legal infrastructure in those areas of business, where the law can support the development of companies. And these more point changes are mostly implemented in the so-called package. A hundred changes, most of which have entered into force this year, while the legislative work we will be discussing today will in turn be carried out with the intention of implementing horizontal or cross-cutting laws. When preparing this package, we tried to look at reality with the eyes of a small entrepreneur. Thus, the solutions are based on five pillars: firstly, strengthening the rights and guarantees for entrepreneurs and providing them with specific legal instruments to safeguard their legitimate interest in general principles, especially in relations with public administration, secondly, increasing legal certainty by protecting the entrepreneur's activities in the event of compliance with, for example, legal explanations or established interpretation practice of the authorities, thirdly, making available an accessible source of knowledge about the rules and procedures and certain practical services on the Entrepreneur's Information Point portal, fourthly, laying down rules for the drafting of draft normative acts concerning economic law, fifthly setting up a spokesman for small and medium-sized entrepreneurs, i.e. a new institution of the nature of institutional principles and rights expressed primarily in the draft Law on Entrepreneurs. As I mentioned, one of the most important challenges facing the Polish economic reality is to build a more partnership between entrepreneurs and administration, because only the relationships based on their cooperation can be built on a lasting foundation for a strong economy. This package continues to act in this direction by introducing in the draft Act the Law of Entrepreneurs, for example, a catalogue of basic principles constituting among others. guidelines, both normative and interpretative, for public authorities. The principle from which the other principles and guarantees are actually derived will play a central role: which is not prohibited by law, which also indicates that an entrepreneur can take all action in the course of his business, unless they are directly prohibited by law. Entrepreneurs will also be supported by the principle of legal certainty, which justifies that the public authority does not depart from the established practice of resolving cases in the same factual and legal situation without a legitimate reason, or other principles: a friendly interpretation of the rules, a settlement of factual doubts or a presumption of fairness. Of course, it can be noted that some of these principles, which I mentioned or which are mentioned in the Law of Entrepreneurs, can be removed from the Constitution, but their explicit position in the Law of Entrepreneurs makes sense. I'll try briefly, but rationally justify it. Firstly, some of these principles are not enshrined in the provisions of the Constitution expressis verbis and have been brought forward by the Constitutional Tribunal only by interpretation, in particular by the democratic rule of law contained in the article. 2 constitutions. Part of this is due to the case law of the Supreme Administrative Court, the Supreme Court or the achievements of the doctrine of law. Therefore, these principles are not always well known to businesses and officials, and their detailed content, scope and legal consequences may raise various doubts in practice. Therefore, such a clear recapitalisation of these principles in the Law of Entrepreneurs has the value of ordering, informational, but also increasing legal awareness and legal certainty. The provisions of the Act will give entrepreneurs reliable, authoritative information about fundamental rights and obligations - especially in his relations with public authorities - on which he will be able to rely directly in these relations. Thanks to the fact that legal awareness will be increased, I think that the actual compliance with economic regulations should be improved at precisely this level, so to speak, derived from the provisions of the basic law, from the Constitution. Secondly, such explicit anchoring of these principles in the Law of Entrepreneurs is an additional, powerful reminder to the public authorities that in their relations with entrepreneurs these principles are fully binding and must be strictly respected, because they are not - I apologize for colloquialism - only the general standards of the Constitution or non-binding appeals of doctrine, are only legal standards and by public authorities, by the administration must be respected as much as all other, very specific, detailed rules, contained either in the Law of Entrepreneurs or in other specific laws. This is extremely important for changing the practice of law enforcement. What emerges to us also as such a reality with talks with entrepreneurs, especially those in these smaller towns, is that, it can be said so jokingly, that when you go to deal with your matter in the office, the reference to the constitution, the case law of the courts, the caselaw of the tribunal or the opinions contained in the law doctrine is not too effective, but the reference to a specific and clear provision of the law - yes. From this point of view, what is the value of specific standards is also that these rules give the officials themselves the basis for such a more partner treatment of entrepreneurs, so that they do not have to circulate around many instances, to go to court to enforce their rights - this level of legal awareness of the courts is of course much higher than the administration - only so that things can be handled positively, because of course there are legal bases for this, already at administrative level, without the need to exploit the judicial path. Thirdly, in the Business Law Act, the principles in question have been adapted in substance to relations relating to the taking-up, pursuit and termination of business activities, adapted in substance to the specifics of business-related matters. This is clearly apparent from the other categories of public administration cases. Fourthly, the provisions of the Business Law which lay down the rules in question also allow for a specific, specific, precise derogation from those rules in particularly justified cases. I would say that it enriches the content of these principles compared to the content that can only be extracted from the Constitution itself. In this way, it can also be said that the right of entrepreneurs creates a constitutionally consistent, but also a coherent system of guarantees, safeguards of economic freedom, including in the form of specific responsibilities of public authorities and the correlated powers of entrepreneurs. The title itself, too, can be said to stress that the centre of this regulation is the entrepreneur and his team of powers, i.e. it can also be said that separate laws relating to entrepreneurs and their rights, also in the sense of such non-legislative ones, are an expression of appreciation for the excellent role of entrepreneurs in economic and social development, but not as a list, I do not know, of declarations, but as a codified team of guarantees and rights, i.e. as such a specific Charter of Entrepreneur's Rights. In addition, thanks to the system we propose, it will be very easy and intuitive to find this law searched by entrepreneurs in the system, however to look at, many different regulations. This will make it an important act from the point of view of the interpretation of the rules relating to entrepreneurs, which should, as I said, affect the sphere of application of the law. In order to ensure that these rules do not remain on paper alone, we propose that the administrative authorities should have specific consequences in breach of these rules. For example, art. 46 Business rights provide that evidence collected in the course of an inspection carried out in violation of these principles - and thus not, as so far, only the rules on the control itself, but also those provisions contained in the entire Business Law Act - cannot constitute evidence in proceedings concerning a controlled trader. With such solutions already more detailed: seeing unfortunately the low survival of entrepreneurs registered in CEIDG, we offer support for those taking the first steps in business. We propose the so-called. the runway, which consists, first, of the so-called operation of the runway. non-registered, which is the answer to often emerging doubts in practice as to when small commercial activities, such as, for example, occasional tutoring or small trade meets the requirements that qualify it as an economic activity. In order to solve such unnecessary problems resulting from this, and also to liberate economic activity of Poles, we have foreseen the solution that the activity of a person who does not earn more than half of the minimum wage for this reason will not be regarded as an economic activity, and consequently will not be subject to an entry into the CEIDG and will therefore not be subject to compulsory social or health insurance for this reason. But what is important, I think, in addition to these economic aspects, is that such a person will not be subject in general to obligations imposed on entrepreneurs in various fields of law today. For example, I do not know, even if environmental law can be indicated here. In our opinion, this will help to reduce the gray area, because this solution is addressed not only to beginner entrepreneurs, but also to people who are not and do not want to be, but they are making, I don't know, to their basic activity, undertaking such a small business activity. These persons will therefore be obliged only and exclusively to keep such simplified sales records for income tax purposes. The second element of this runway is the so-called runway. It's a start-up relief. This is an optional exemption for beginners from social security contributions for the first 6 months of their business activity. This means that the entrepreneur will have to report to compulsory social security within 6 months of the start of business and not, as at present, 7 days. After applying for insurance, the entrepreneur will, of course, be entitled, as at present, to this 24-month preferential premium period, i.e. the so-called. Little ZUS. This is also a solution for start-ups, who need to start at the beginning of this run-up, time to organize a workshop, start earning revenue. This solution is certainly the most cost-intensive for the state, because on the one hand it will bring almost 8 billion savings for companies in 10 years, but on the other hand it is the income from which the State is giving up just in return for increasing the economic activity of people wondering today whether they want to undertake this activity or not, and what I said at the beginning, the survival of such entrepreneurs. Other things... Legal certainty. Today 70% the applicable law is the result of the implementation of Union law. Not always creating simple, unambiguous regulations... This, of course, is always an object, so to speak, desire and discussion, conversation, but making such adjustments is not always possible from the point of view of the reality that is being regulated, because it is often a complicated reality. That is why we are here implementing or proposing solutions that will strengthen the sense of legal certainty and enable businesses to move safer in this complex regulatory environment. That is why we propose a new institution in the Law of Entrepreneurs or in this public economic law, which is legal explanations, i.e. clear, written in a simple language of translation of particularly complex legal provisions with the presentation of practical aspects of these provisions. An entrepreneur who complies with such legal considerations will not be able to bear the negative consequences. A similar function is to comply with the established practice of interpretation of offices. In total, these two solutions create a kind of legal certainty clause. It has been in force since January of this year in tax matters, because the deregulation law, which entered into force on 1 January, introduced this solution in the Tax Order. Here we extend its application to other areas of business. What is also pointed out in the project is that entrepreneurs are increasingly using modern technologies in everyday life. Therefore, there are no reasons why these technologies could not be used to deal with administrative matters. Hence, we propose to extend the catalogue of instruments by which it will be possible to communicate on the office line - a party in the course of proceedings, e.g. about telephone or e-mail communication. For example, the Office will be able to inform about the need to provide missing information or documents, which is instead of sending a registered letter, which is used most often today, as part of a call to remedy the deficiencies, or even in the case of minor cases, it will be possible to deal with the matter in its entirety. Of course, this is optional, and the entrepreneur will have to agree to it, with this change - because we propose it to be introduced into the CC. - they will also be able to benefit non-business citizens. With regard to the suspension of economic activity, we are also proposing some flexibility here. What is, I think, important from such a social point of view is that the economic activity will be suspended by an entrepreneur who employs a staff member on maternity or parental leave. Why? Now the requirement, we know, to be able to suspend ourselves, is not to employ workers, and this is particularly important in the context of hiring women who... Such a barrier to the employment of women, because an entrepreneur, doing small-scale activities, not knowing whether this activity will develop or whether he will have to carry it out on this smaller scale, will not employ... Today we are hearing about this problem, that young women are not particularly employed because of the potential future maternity or parental leave, because in this case the entrepreneur today cannot suspend this activity. We are also abolishing the limitation of the suspension period. Today is 24 months. It will be possible to suspend for any period indicated by the trader, but it will also be possible to suspend indefinitely. This is a reduction of the kind of fiction we have today. In fact, the law today a little bit to say, provokes people to circumvent the rules, because if we have this 2-year maximum suspension period, what does it look like in practice for people who do not want to return to business after 2 years? They hang up on Tuesday, they hang back on Wednesday. So I don't think the state should cultivate a certain fiction. As far as CEIDG is concerned, we are also developing some functionality of records here. Such a very practical... I don't know, it's like... sending notifications to traders, for example, about the impending end of the suspension period, etc. As far as the catalogue of forms of regulation is concerned, we are here, first of all, introducing a clear catalogue of three types or forms of regulation, i.e. concessions, permits and register of regulated activities. Thus, we abolish consents and licenses as separate forms. At the same time, we highlight the differences between concessions and permits, in particular to make it clear that the authorisation cannot be discretionary. In other words, if the trader fulfils the objective conditions for obtaining the authorisation, the licensing authority shall not be able to refuse the authorisation. As for control, we are not making major changes to the current legal situation, because we introduced major systemic changes a year ago. There was a debate here, too. The amendments entered into force on 1 January, i.e. checks carried out on the basis of a risk analysis, i.e. less checks - I apologise for the blindness, regulation of sampling procedures, the possibility to lodge a complaint on the chronicity of checks, the possibility to carry out joint checks where they are mandatory, so that the trader can request the authorities to join the audit, the introduction of a ban on re-checking or the obligation of the authorities to include information on the control procedures on the websites. Before today's debate, we looked at the effects of these changes, because I remember that not only during the debate in the Sejm, but also from the information we received during the consultation, there was such concern that there are too many exemptions from those changes that we have made. On the other hand, we have looked through the prism of two audit institutions, i.e. the National Tax Administration and the Social Security Office, and the figures show that there has been a decrease in the number of checks while at the same time increasing the effectiveness and effectiveness of these checks. So we're achieving the exact goal that we assumed at the time: there's less control, but we have a better shot, so to speak, of these controls. So entrepreneurs who do not cause their operation, the action of some over-vigilance of control authorities, are less controlled today. On the other hand, inspections are more frequent in areas where this risk increases. For this reason, we do not make great revolutions in this chapter, although a few changes are made, e.g. prohibit the use in subsequent proceedings of evidence obtained in breach of general principles, the possibility of objecting to the infringement of the prohibition of re-checking or the introduction of clear rules of opposition in a complaint against checks carried out in breach of law. We propose to add in Business Law a chapter on the basic principles for drafting draft normative acts in the field of economic law. It is a catalogue of guidelines that should be guided by the design of projects, with guidelines for those who prepare projects. These are not rules, I would say, procedural, but provisions addressed to people who are preparing projects. They are mainly focused on the deregulation aspect, because here the issues of proportionality of burdens, including burdens on SMEs, or on the implementation of European law are highlighted, but they are also about monitoring tools, mandatory deregulation of all ministers whose results will be submitted to the Council of Ministers. Business people will be supported by the Internet portal Information Point for Entrepreneur. This will be the development of the current website www.biznes.gov.pl, which will not only explain how to handle individual official matters, but will also enable e.g. payment of official fees through this portal or obtaining a certificate of non-payment with taxes or ZUS contributions. The portal will also provide clear, precise information on the rules on the pursuit of business activities. The point will use some basic communication technologies, e.g. will remind entrepreneurs through SMS or e-mail about the upcoming official dates. The website will also publish explanations on legal issues that raise the most doubts in practice. It will also be possible to submit proposals for concrete improvements or removal of administrative burdens through this official portal. At the same time, the act or the draft law on central register will also introduce a number of changes aimed at improving the functionality of the register itself. It will also implement solutions that meet the needs of entrepreneurs and other users of this register that we have recently collected. For example, such practical functionality will be the possibility of posting information in the CEIDG about the proxy, which will be equivalent to the granting of a power of attorney in writing if, of course, there is no specific form required in the situation. Such a very practical change concerning single-person companies is the possibility of giving procura. From a practical point of view, this is a very important, practical improvement institution, I think, to run a company whose smallest entrepreneurs have so far been deprived to date. There is in principle no justification for single-member companies not to benefit from this, while companies registered in the National Court Register may. Thus, the trader will no longer have to issue a detailed mandate for any activity exceeding the ordinary management. Moreover, it will not really be necessary to wonder whether it is a simple board of directors, whether it is in excess of the ordinary board of directors or whether the power of attorney is in some way limited. So, from the point of view of the contractors of such an entrepreneur and the certainty of trading, this is also a very important solution. The key issue that dictated the design of the package was also the need to ensure the special protection of the interests and rights of entrepreneurs in the SME sector, but institutional protection. We know that these entrepreneurs represent almost 99% all companies, while producing 50% GDP. On the other hand, due to their specific characteristics, they are, of course, most often deprived of their facilities in the form of e.g., I don't know, extensive legal departments, most often they are alone, unfortunately quite moderate, and therefore also stand on a weakened position in contacts or administration or with stronger contractors. I would even compare the situation of such a lowest trader with that of the consumer. Because when it comes to legal awareness, about moving around in this complicated reality, I think this is a very, very comparable situation. For this reason, it should also be crucial in our view to establish an ombudsman for small and medium-sized enterprises, who is about to uphold the rights of these smallest entrepreneurs. It can be said that these smallest entrepreneurs are not currently associated with employers' organisations or that there is a very low level of this association. In my view, therefore, they are often deprived of institutional protection. On the other hand, as we have checked the experiences of other countries, e.g. The United States, South Korea, Australia or Georgia, i.e. countries that have such very modern regulations, because regulations in all these countries seem to be such ones, I can say that in these countries there is such an institution, more - it is highly appreciated by entrepreneurs, by experts, and even by the administration itself. We have tried to exchange experiences with these countries. And that's kind of the role of a lawyer in relations with public administration, for example. It can also be said - as is the case in other countries - that this improves the investment climate at all or around businesses. Besides, I think that there is also a positive example of such an institution in Poland. It is a financial spokesman who, every year - seems - accepts about 15 thousand. whether complaints and requests. Of course, in order for the Ombudsman to be able to carry out his tasks effectively, he must also have competence both in the systemic, horizontal and in solving the problems of specific entrepreneurs. Therefore, in these two spheres he will have these powers concerning e.g. opinions on legal acts. This opinion will be circling whether or not it will be attached to the project. During the entire legislative process, he will be able to demand legal clarification by individual ministers or an evaluation of the functioning of individual legal acts, but he will also have such intervention powers. So we think that this can also further induce foreigners living in Poland or foreign investors who do not formulate their business by registering in the National Court Register, to increase economic activity in our country. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u429	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The project package submitted by the Minister for Development and Finance, consisting of the Business Constitution, provides for the adoption of five laws, which will regulate in a comprehensive and consistent manner the general principles of doing business in Poland. These regulations, creating favourable, transparent and stable business conditions and strengthening the guarantees of freedom and business rights, reduce business risks and increase the willingness of entrepreneurs to take up and pursue business activities. It should be noted that these projects are undoubtedly deregulating in the area of economic law, due to both their conciseness and factuality, as well as the introduction of new material-law solutions extending the scope of freedom of economic activity. The purpose of the Business Constitution package is to fully implement the constitutional principle of freedom of business and other constitutional principles relevant to entrepreneurs and their business activity. The basis for achieving this objective is the draft Law on Business Law, intended to replace the current Law on the Freedom of Business, which, as a result of numerous amendments, no longer pursues its basic objective, which is to establish a set of principles of economic law. The right of entrepreneurs in a clear and transparent manner will not only regulate the taking up and pursuit of business activity in Poland, but also introduce a number of standards of a guarantee nature for entrepreneurs and rules preventing excessive interference of public authorities in constitutionally guaranteed freedom of business and other rules concerning entrepreneurs. The rules binding on economic operators include the principle of freedom of business, Article. 2 The law, the principle of which is not prohibited, is permitted, the obligation to conduct business in accordance with the principles of fair competition and respect for good practices and the legitimate interests of other traders and consumers, as well as the respect and protection of human rights and freedoms. At the same time, the principles binding on public authorities in their relations with entrepreneurs include: the principle of freedom of establishment and equality of entrepreneurs, the principle of which is not prohibited by law, the principle of presumption of integrity of the trader, the principle of resolving factual doubts in favour of the trader, the principle of a friendly interpretation of the rules, the principle of increasing trust, proportionality, impartiality and equal treatment, the principle of liability of officials for infringements of the law, the principle of legal certainty, the principle of providing information. At the same time, the draft contains provisions which indicate specific consequences for the authorities in the event of breach of the above principles. The solution proposed in a project worthy of special distinction is the introduction of non-registered institutions. The project provides that a smaller-scale natural person - i.e. monthly revenue accruing from it shall not exceed 50% The minimum wage, i.e. in 2018 1050 PLN - does not have to register this activity or pay ZUS contributions for this purpose. This will be possible provided that this person has not carried out business activity in the last 60 months. At the same time, the Business Law provides for a certain type of advantage and incentive for entrepreneurs entering into business for the first time or again after a longer period of time. Namely introduces the so-called. a start-up relief that start-ups will not have to pay social security contributions during the first 6 months of their business activity. After this period, the entrepreneur will be able to benefit additionally from the current relief, i.e. the so-called. a small ZUS, over the next 24 months. The draft Law on Business Law, which deserves to be underlined, provides for the administration to issue legal explanations written in a simple language explaining the rules on economic activity in terms of practical application. The project also clearly defines the catalogue of forms of regulation of economic activities - concessions, permits, entries in the register of regulated activities - and provides for the abolition of consents and licences as separate forms of regulation of that activity. In addition, it is envisaged to make the suspension institution more flexible, inter alia. the possibility of permanent suspension of economic activity. The aim of the business constitution of the Small and Medium-sized Entrepreneurs' Ombudsman Act is to support and monitor the proper implementation of the Business Constitution in practice. In particular, the spokesperson for small and medium-sized enterprises is to work towards implementing the principles of freedom of business, enhancing the confidence of entrepreneurs in public authority, impartiality and equal treatment, sustainable development and fair competition and respect for good practices and the legitimate interests of entrepreneurs. The Ombudsman will uphold the rights of micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. He will be appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers at the request of the Minister responsible for Economic Affairs for a 6-year term of office and will actively take action at that time to protect the interests of entrepreneurs. It will be equipped with instruments suitable for wider action, for entrepreneurs in general. For example, he will give an opinion on draft legislation on economic activity, have proposed a legislative initiative, have sought to resolve differences in the interpretation of the law or to issue legal explanations or have informed the competent authorities of the identified irregularities and barriers to the conduct of business. The Ombudsman will also be equipped with intervention powers, such as the possibility of asking public authorities, organisations or institutions to provide explanations, to provide information or to make available files and documents, the right to request administrative proceedings, to lodge a complaint with the administrative court and to participate in those proceedings on the rights of the prosecutor, to assist in the organisation of mediation between a micro, small or medium-sized enterprise and an administrative body. A spokesperson for small and medium-sized enterprises will therefore guarantee institutional principles and rights expressed in the Business Law Act. Another project from the Business Constitution package is the bill on Central Registration and Information about Business Activity and Information Point for the Entrepreneur. It replaces the existing provisions of the Central Register and Information on Business Activity and the rules governing the operation of a single point of contact contained in the Freedom of Business Act. At the same time, the draft law aims to enable information on the rights granted by economic operators and the procurents established to be made available in the Central Register and Information on Economic Activity to be made available, to make a new breakdown of the data entered in the register into the register of the economic operator's records and information about the economic operator, to introduce the principle of automatic resumption of business activity after the end of the period of suspension specified by the economic operator, to implement the principle of the right of establishment to be suspended by economic operators - natural persons - to carry out business activities for an indefinite period, while the current period is in principle acceptable up to 24 months. At the same time, with regard to the Entrepreneur's Information Point, the project is mainly aimed at facilitating the use of functionalities enabling the implementation of cases by electronic means via this point, e.g. by means of the possibility to re-use previously entered into the data system, and to clarify the tasks of the authorities which carry out the cases or may provide practical information on issues relating to the taking up, pursuit and termination of business activities. The main purpose of the draft law on the rules for participation of foreign entrepreneurs and other foreign persons in trade in the territory of the Republic of Poland is to regulate as comprehensively as possible the rules for participation of foreign entrepreneurs and other foreign persons in trade in the territory of the Republic of Poland. This objective will be achieved by bringing together in a single law and harmonising the provisions implementing the Services Directive in the territory of the Republic of Poland and the provisions of the Law on freedom of economic activity concerning foreign persons, branches and representatives of foreign entrepreneurs. In the Law on the Freedom of Economic Activity in force since 2004, regulations relating to foreign entrepreneurs are only partially mixed among standards of a very different nature, often not involving foreign entrepreneurs or other foreign persons. At the same time, the existing law on the provision of services in the territory of the Republic of Poland, so-called. the service law requires certain changes resulting from the experience of its application since 2010. In addition to maintaining existing solutions, the bill introduces a number of changes that are beneficial for entrepreneurs and the certainty of economic turnover. In addition, the project shall include in the application a document confirming the authorisation of the person indicated in the application to represent the foreign trader in the representation, together with confirmation of the acceptance of that authorisation by the authorised person. Thanks to the creation of a new, consistent regulation, it is possible to repeal two laws: the Law of 4 March 2010 on the provision of services in the territory of the Republic of Poland and the Archaic Law of 6 July 1982 on the principles of conducting business activity in the field of small manufacturing in the territory of the Polish People's Republic by foreign legal and natural persons. Given the need to make numerous changes to a number of separate laws, as well as to lay down extensive rules - transitional and adjustment regulations - which are a consequence of the adoption of the Business Law Act and the other four laws constituting the Business Constitution package, it is appropriate to regulate the above. matter in the Law Rules introducing the Law of Entrepreneurs and other laws concerning business activities. The introduction of the Business Constitution package requires the revision of a number of specific provisions on references to definitions, institutions and solutions which are in the Law of Businesses and other laws, including relief for start-ups and non-registration activities and the appointment of a spokesperson for small and medium-sized enterprises. There are also provisions governing references to the rules for carrying out checks on changes related to the replacement of the obligation of the trader to use part of the offices with the number REGON or in parallel the number REGON and NIP for the sole purpose of the NIP number, the regulation of economic activities, in particular the transfer to separate general provisions contained so far in the Act on the Freedom of Business, as well as the replacement of approvals and licences provided for in the Act on the Freedom of Business by Authorisations, which aims to simplify and consolidate the system of conducting business activities in Poland. It was also replaced by the following acts of reference to the Polish People's Republic by the phrase: Rzeczpospolita Polska. The draft law Introductory provisions also contains an amendment to the Code of Administrative Procedure for the deformity of communication on the line office - entrepreneur. With the consent or at the request of the trader, official cases or individual actions, e.g. Calls to remedy formal deficiencies will be handled by telephone, e-mail or other means of communication. The next amendment concerns the possibility for the parties to conduct an evaluation of the functioning of public administrations, including the staff of public administrations. At the same time, a well-accepted revision of the Civil Code by the business community, aimed at empowering entrepreneurs - individuals to provide procura after obtaining an entry in the Central Register and Information on Economic Activity. In the light of the above-mentioned facts, the business constitution is to be a positive, pro-free break in business relations with public administrations, allowing for the liberation of Polish entrepreneurship and the restoration of its proper public administration, I stress, serviceable towards citizens, serviceable towards entrepreneurs of the role. As this package is comprehensive, it contains a number of regulations and I think that such a holistic view shows the consistency of all these bill projects, the PiS positively reviews the draft laws presented and asks for further proceding of the business constitution. Thank you very much, Marshal, for your attention.

Let's look at all the most important changes that the law introduces for the Polish entrepreneur from the package of constitutions for business. Rules of law. The main assumptions of these changes are to complement the existing Polish law-making directives requiring the legislator, in theory the State, to follow certain principles: to seek or impose new administrative obligations only to the extent necessary to achieve the objective; to seek to reduce information obligations; to make it possible to carry out information obligations in electronic form; to seek only to impose administrative obligations necessary to achieve the objectives of European Union and international law; to make a mandatory assessment of the impact of the proposed change on businesses; to monitor the functioning of the law by making its ongoing assessment. The proposed principles of law-making are an expression of the idea of minimum legislative intervention by the State in economic freedom and law-making in a careful and mature manner. However, the effectiveness of these solutions may be limited as the provisions introduced do not constitute innovation. These are solutions known from other laws which failed to protect Polish legislation from these problems. Reduction of control of economic activities. With regard to the restriction of control of business activities, the Business Constitution introduces provisions similar to those which entered into force on 1 January 2017. In the course of the work, even the only envisaged convenience to abolish the documentary obligation of keeping a control book was abandoned. Since the beginning of this year, the government has introduced principles that are beneficial for entrepreneurs - e.g. providing that the audit can only be carried out after an analysis of the likelihood of an infringement by the trader or prohibiting the same authority from re-checking the already controlled scope - but provided for so many exceptions that in practice they do not guarantee any protection for businesses. Therefore, such a very desirable restriction of control remains only theoretical. The relief on the start is a positive effect, an aspect. New definition of economic activity. The new definition omits the generic criterion, indicating only the specific characteristics which should be characterised by the activity in question in order to be considered as an economic activity. In practice, however, it does not bring any benefit to entrepreneurs, like most changes to the announced constitution for business. Unregistered activities, a new form of activity - on the plus side as much as possible. On the other hand, when it comes to starting, doing and suspending activities, here the assessment is negative. With regard to the start-up, execution and suspension of economic activities, the proposed changes are not far-reaching. An interesting solution seems to be the above-mentioned start-up relief, while it should be noted that the conditions for obtaining it are similar to the conditions for taking advantage of the already functioning so-called start-up relief. Little ZUS. On the other hand, the possibility of unlimited suspension of activities by persons operating on the basis of an entry in the central register of economic activities should be assessed negatively. Firstly, it is incomprehensible to deprive other traders of this right. Secondly, it is not desirable to allow the business to be suspended for more than 2 years, which limits the transparency of economic turnover, especially since the entry and deletion of the trader is easy and currently free of charges, so if the trader does not intend to continue in the near future, it should be deleted from that register. Extension of rights to establish a proxy - as positive as possible. Legal explanations. It seems like an interesting aspect, but that's not the case if we look at practice. The Business Constitution provides for the introduction of a new institution: legal explanations. They are to be written in simple language instructions for entrepreneurs on the understanding and application of the rules. Explanations are to be issued by central authorities, in particular ministers. An entrepreneur wishing to protect himself against criminal or administrative sanctions will have to check the legal explanations and follow up individual interpretations issued in a similar factual situation during a given settlement period and 12 months before it starts and then follow the prevailing view. At first glance, it seems that explanations will make it easier for entrepreneurs. However, it is worth noting that in addition to the law itself, the entrepreneur should also keep up with interpretations and key judgments. This will now come to the conclusion of the legal explanations issued by the ministers. It therefore appears that the introduction of another institution to facilitate the movement of legislation in this maze may have a counterproductive effect. Explanations will not solve the problem of overproduction of law and the legislator should focus on creating clear and stable rules rather than introducing further legislation. In conclusion, the business constitution presented by Mr Mateusz Morawiecki seems to meet expectations. Most of its theoretical assumptions are commendable, but in practice these laws introduce very cosmetic changes which may not fulfil their function in economic trade. The business constitution does not constitute a new quality in relation to the current regulations. Moreover, even the best revision of the rules will not result in representatives of the state administration starting to treat entrepreneurs in partnership, and the current practice of the state authorities does not give hope for a real change for entrepreneurs. Non-existent legislation is an obstacle to the business-friendly partnership of officials. All of these aspects make the whole business constitution not much work. And it won't change even the best business Constitution. We need a new law in Poland to measure Wilczek's law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Well, unfortunately, it is. Offices towards entrepreneurs are to proceed openly, provide information, maintain the principle of proportionality, rationality, do not depart without clear reasons from established standards of conduct. Fixed interpretation practice - offices are obliged to issue individual interpretations at the request of the entrepreneur. If the trader complies with the interpretation, he cannot be held liable administratively, financially or criminally. These are all good and necessary solutions. Leviathan, the Polish Crafts Association, but also OPZZ. This could lead to distortions of competition in the multi-service market, and micro- and small businesses will be the main victims. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u466	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the package of government billings called contractually the Business Constitution, which includes the following draft laws: Business Law, Print No 2051, on the Ombudsman of Small and Medium Entrepreneurs, Print No 2052, on the Central Register and Information on Business Activity and Information Point for Entrepreneur, Print No 2053, on the rules of participation of foreign entrepreneurs and other foreign persons in economic trade in the territory of the Republic of Poland, Print No 2054 and the Rules introducing the Act of Business Law and other laws concerning business activity, Print No 2055. The draft Law on Business Law is a central act of economic law which is intended to replace the existing law of 2 July 2004 on freedom of business. The aim of the law is to implement the constitutional principle of freedom of economic activity. The amendments provide for a catalogue of rules which all public authorities will have to comply with when dealing with matters in which entrepreneurs will be party. A favourable attitude towards entrepreneurs will be to act in accordance with the principles: which is not prohibited by law, is allowed, trust in the entrepreneur and presumption of his integrity, resolve factual doubts in favour of an economic unit, a friendly interpretation of the rules or an amicable settlement of disputes. The key document of the Business Constitution is based, among others, on: Introduction of the so-called non-registered activities, i.e. activities not disclosed in the Central Register and Information on Economic Activity, and carried out by a natural person from whom income does not exceed 50% in any month the amount of the minimum remuneration. Consequently, this will mean exemption from social security contributions for these entities. On the other hand, the solutions referred to as start-up services refer to the implementation of the start-up business support instrument. The proposed rules provide for the exemption of new entrepreneurs for a period of 6 months from the obligation to pay social security contributions. In turn for the next 2 years they will be subject to the so-called regulations. Little ZUS. Other key proposals included in the project include:: 1. the establishment of a catalogue of rights and obligations of business and offices, which provide interpretation guidelines for bodies applying economic law - these are fundamental legal principles relating to business relations - administration; 2) a more friendly and partner-friendly relationship between administration and entrepreneurs to date - it is about introducing, among others. the principles of the presumption of integrity of the trader, the principles of the settlement of factual doubts in favour of the trader and a friendly interpretation of the rules, as well as the principles of cooperation between the authorities in the conduct of business matters; 3. abolishing the obligation for entrepreneurs to use the REGON number in relations with offices - the trader in contacts with a large part of the offices will have to use only the NIP number and this number will serve these offices to identify it, REGON will be gradually withdrawn from the market; 4. the elimination of consents and licences as separate forms of regulation of economic activities; 5. launch of the Entrepreneur Information Point, which will provide comprehensive information on the regulated activities; 6) making the institutions of suspension of economic activity more flexible - among other things: the possibility of suspending the pursuit of economic activities indefinitely or for any period; 7. the establishment of an ombudsman for small and medium-sized entrepreneurs - it will be an intervention body and a guardian body for entrepreneurs, with competence e.g. to apply to the Offices with requests for legal explanations. The proposed solutions will improve the operating conditions of businesses and reduce the risk of skipping the basic principles of economic law when the administration takes specific decisions on individual business cases. The expected effect should also be to change the attitude of administrative authorities towards entrepreneurs and strengthen the role of administration, including by taking greater account of the legitimate interests of economic operators. The new rules will help to reduce the grey economy and encourage young and entrepreneurial Poles to start their own business. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! All draft laws within the framework of the "Business Constitution" package are very important and form a whole, but it is still worth paying particular attention to the draft law on the rules for the participation of foreign entrepreneurs and other foreign persons in economic trade in the territory of the Republic of Poland, which meets the need to merge in a single legal act provisions concerning cross-border service activities, branches and representations of foreign entrepreneurs and other rules for the participation of foreign entrepreneurs and other foreign persons in economic trade in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The project is important and regulates legal regulations in an important area of activity for domestic and foreign entrepreneurs in the context of European integration and a globalised economy. The business constitution is an extremely important package for the development of entrepreneurship and the enhancement of economic freedom in Poland, which introduces a whole list of fundamental rights, facilitations and reliefs for entrepreneurs. We are convinced that they will influence the growth of economic activity of citizens, and thus will stimulate the development of the small and medium-sized enterprises sector as well as the economy as a whole, contributing to the growth of Poland's economic competitiveness in the global market. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle strongly supports the set of draft laws prepared by the Deputy Prime Minister, the Minister of Development and Finance of Mateusz Morawiecki, known as the Business Constitution.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u474	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I submit a report to the Committee on Culture and Media on the Government's draft law amending the Act on the organisation and pursuit of cultural activities from print no. 2057. In the justification of the project we read: One of the basic objectives of the Act on the organisation and pursuit of cultural activities is to define the way in which cultural entities are financed. The sources of funding provided under this aid are different, but in any case they must comply with the provisions of European Union law on the granting of State aid. The admissibility of public aid in culture is governed by Article. 107 and 108 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. According to these provisions, aid for culture may have been granted by 2014 after prior notification and approval by the European Commission. In 2014 the Council The Commission has simplified the procedures for granting public aid in culture by including this sector in a Commission regulation declaring certain types of aid compatible with the internal market. This means that, once the conditions laid down in that Regulation have been met, aid for culture is exempted from notification. In the current legal situation, the above mechanism was used only for aid for culture and the protection of cultural heritage under the Operational Programme "Infrastructure and the environment" for the period 2014-2020. In practice, aid for cultural activities is also granted under other major programmes not covered by Union support programmes. This applies in particular to the Norwegian Financial Mechanism and the Financial Mechanism of the European Economic Area. The funds transferred to beneficiaries under these mechanisms are also subject to the rules of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union and it is therefore necessary to introduce rules facilitating the implementation of public aid procedures also in the case of these financial mechanisms. It is therefore proposed to introduce an article into the Act. 28a authorising the establishment of aid schemes which will be the basis for the granting of public aid to individual operators. The scope of these aid schemes has been broadly established by reference to each programme established under the Agreement between the European Union and the State or Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On 7 December this year, the Committee on Culture and Media carried out its first reading of this draft law and examined it by amending it. On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I request that the High House adopt the draft attached to the report. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u475	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Mirosław Stachowiak-Rózecka is the first to speak. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u476	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed law is adapted to European Union law. By 2014 - the rapporteur mentioned this - the issue of public aid for culture was regulated Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. On the basis of the article. 7 of this Treaty, Member States were able to grant public aid in the field of culture after prior notification and agreement. It was tedious and long-term, and therefore in 2014 the European Commission decided to simplify the procedure by integrating the cultural sector into Regulation No 651/2014 of the European Commission, which makes it possible to grant public aid without having to be notified to the European Commission. In order to benefit from this Regulation, the Minister for Culture and National Heritage may develop a programme of assistance in the rank of the Regulation. The proposed amendment to the Act on the organisation and pursuit of cultural activities aims at empowering the Minister of Culture and National Heritage to issue a Regulation as an aid scheme, within the meaning of Regulation No 651/2014 of the European Commission on block exemptions, to projects from the cultural sector which will receive grants from the Financial Mechanism of the European Economic Area. The expected amount of cultural funding from this mechanism in the current financial perspective is EUR 75 million. Thus, in short, thanks to this law, and as a consequence, the regulation will simply make it possible to obtain EU funds more quickly for culture, which is why, of course, the Law and Justice Club supports the draft amendment to the Act on the organisation and pursuit of cultural activities. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-07-sejm-53-2.u483	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the law on the organisation and pursuit of cultural activities. Let me remind you that the Act of 25 October 1991 is the basic act currently governing the area of cultural activity, in particular how it is financed. The amendment of the law results from the absence of a statutory mandate for the Minister of Culture and National Heritage to issue regulations on public aid for projects from the cultural sector implemented under the Agreement between the European Union and the Member States or the Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement. In the current state of law, the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage may only issue regulations governing the provision of public aid in projects co-financed by the European Regional Development Fund. The Act does not regulate the distribution of other funds, inter alia. assistance provided by a Member State or Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement on the basis of an agreement with the European Union. The Minister for Culture and National Heritage shall ensure that his financial support is compatible with the provisions of European Union law on State aid. It is therefore necessary to authorise the Minister responsible for cultural and national heritage protection to issue implementing acts governing the rules on State aid. Therefore, the project promoters propose to add an article. 28a on the basis of which the Minister will be authorised to issue regulations which are aid schemes to be notified to the European Commission without notification. The amendment shall apply to any programme implemented under the Agreement between the European Union and the Member States or the Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement. The regulation introduced by this law is optional and its application will depend on the availability of non-refundable funds transferred to Poland on the basis of contracts. Importantly, the Regulation will take into account all sources of public support. It should be stressed that the aid will be provided for both investment projects and projects involving cultural and artistic events. The funds transferred to the protection of cultural heritage and the development of infrastructure and the associated increase in the number of events organised in my opinion will contribute to increasing citizens' access to a wider culture, which will consequently translate into a general improvement of living standards and social inclusion. It is also important that new projects will also create jobs. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding the bill.

On behalf of the inhabitants of Włocławek and the former Włocławski voivodship, I would like to thank the Government of Law and Justice, the government of good change for two important investment decisions that we have been waiting for for many years, made recently. The first one is the integration into the railway station investment programme for the years 2016-2023, the railway station in Włocławek. I direct my thanks to the Minister of Infrastructure and Construction of Mr Andrzej Adamczyk and the President of PKP Mr Krzysztof Mamiński, who on 26 October this year. informed about the addition of the station's investment programme covering nearly 200 facilities and providing them with funding. For the modernization of the facility, the passengers and users of the station waited for many years. The second good news is the decision to build a water level on the Vistula Vistula in Sierzewo, which - after 47 years of independent functioning of the water barrier in Włocławek - will finally ensure the protection of my region against flooding and energy, will raise tourism value and provide the jobs sought. The water level in Włocławek is an important flood protection infrastructure at the national level, and its construction from the beginning assumed the formation of further water levels under the cascade of the lower Vistula. In agreement concluded on 1 December this year. between the Minister of the Environment, the Minister of Energy and the Minister of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, we read: The planned construction of the water level Sierzewo is the most advanced project under the cascade of the Lower Vistula, whose implementation would eliminate the critical risk of a disaster which could result from damage to the water level in Włocławek. In view of the above, the Government of the Republic of Poland qualifies the construction of the water level of Sierzewo as a priority water project on a national scale, which should be carried out within the framework of public tasks of the State, no later than by the end of 2025. What had been impossible for the other rulers over the years for the Government of Law and Justice turned out to be possible. I am pleased that my writings, interpelations, talks with the ministers of infrastructure and construction, environment, energy, water and inland waterway management and co-organised conferences have proved necessary and effective. As a native Italian woman, I thank all those who supported the actions leading to the decision on the implementation of these key investments for my city and region. In a special way, I would like to thank Mr Kosmie Złotowski, Member of the European Parliament for his actions in favour of the region. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
